A registration/proof-of-possession game over `StoredAccept` proves conditional attribution, shows storage is not possession, and retains the dropped-`KeyBinding` counterexample. A concrete challenge-response PoP protocol signs `popTag ++ channelId ++ nonce`; fixed-width validity makes the encoding injective, the session-bound verifier recomputes it from the verifier's challenge, and honest completeness, wave-9 `StoredRegistration` composition, and conditional funding attribution are proved. `PopKeySoundOn` is the weakest law used; unbound-challenge, nonce-free, no-`Valid`, and reused-challenge replay are mechanised counterexamples, with `EufCma` plus freshness identified as the preventing hypotheses. No real signature scheme or computational EUF-CMA claim is made. |

LnLean.Proofs.Channel.CoverageSourceTargets.no_wire_witness_authenticator; LnLean.Proofs.Channel.CoverageSourceTargets.coverage_source_summary` | Under a BOLT 3-shaped serializer with an exact transaction wire layout, an explicit signing law, and the commitment value equations, commitment_signed covers exactly the current HTLC set and fee and its digest/signatures are derived under C3; digest binding implies view binding only under explicit collision-free/serialization hypotheses, and the wire record alone determines neither; the commitment-wire preimage is byte-equal to the vendored BIP 143 encoder, with the HTLC instantiation still open. The eight arbitrary-preimage field hypotheses are explicit and complete; conditional byte/digest/signature transfer is mechanized, dropped-locktime/amount counterexamples are retained, and wire-only legacy coverage remains negative. The missing legacy-wire data is characterized as a per-HTLC coverage witness: the canonical witness decodes exactly to the full record's coverage, no legacy-only decoder or wire-compatible witness selector is correct for all full records, the amount row is necessary, and the same legacy message admits distinct decoding witnesses. A production decoder therefore requires an out-of-band/full-record source or a protocol change. |
